## 15. SEC6: write boundaries, returned privacy and deployment review

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-21, commissioned by wave-21 SEC6. EVOL owns
adoption, rollback fidelity, migration expressiveness and release preview narration.
Section 14 is reserved for SEC5, integrating concurrently on main.
This amendment adds analysis and client validation; runtime authorization stays
with the existing rules. No language spelling or authority exemption is added.

### 15.1 Every executed write

Column `write only` intent covers insert, insert-if-absent's inserted arm,
update, and both upsert arms. `update only` remains intentionally edit-only;
creation may legitimately have different authority. Missing intent advice asks
about an authored insert/upsert path whose admission does not establish its
update restriction. It is a conditional review question, never a refusal or an
inferred product specification. Operation requirements and literal/default
payloads narrow the advice where the implemented reduction establishes them.

Finite history challenges must observe actual executed write boundaries, not
infer them from a net before/after image. Opt-in, host-only fixture instrumentation
judges supported independent policy predicates immediately before each physical
insert/update/delete against that transaction's current snapshot. Updates judge
old and next images; column updates (whether targeted by `write only` or `update only`) require an actual column change. No-op and
if-absent collision arms are not inserted writes. The trace includes intermediate
writes subsequently overwritten, loops, and delete/reinsert. Only a committed
ordinary authored operation contributes failure-eligible evidence; a rolled-back
operation contributes no write violation. Seed/synthetic origins retain their
non-failing classification. Unknown/error judgments are coverage gaps.
Instrumentation has a separate reader and meter, cannot alter product admission,
and is unavailable on the wire and the web device. No finite trace proves intent.
The hook is Context::with_fixture_trace, absent by default and not deserialized.
The physical insert/replace/delete primitives invoke it after authorization and
validation, before their store write. Collision/no-op branches never reach it;
upsert arm identity follows the primitive actually executed, not write_tables.
It creates a fresh Runner over the same provisional transaction with a separate
unbounded meter, authorization budget, caches and observation state. It does not
change the application Runner or serialize anything into its outcome. A Created
predicate is unknown until created-fact instrumentation is supported. The caller
discards the entire trace unless mutate returns Sent. Policy errors never alter
the product result. Implicit effects without an instrumented boundary remain
explicitly uncovered. Such missing hooks do not invalidate neighboring instantaneous
judgments, but every cross-boundary stability claim inherits §12.8's transitive
implicit-target and read-authority-dependency closure. No new kernel axiom.

### 15.2 Mutation results are observations

Column privacy includes implicit write results, explicit return objects and
authored refusals. A query projection is only one observer's shape. Located
no-invariant advice identifies columns omitted from an authored query projection
or guarded by a column rule that another authored result/refusal may observe.
It names the operation and column and asks for independent spec intent; it does
not claim that every returned private column violates the intended audience.

Actual compiler-typed mutation row payloads are checked against column read
intent as queries are. This reduction requires a stable necessary policy across
the mutation and a surviving committed image; ambiguous intermediate/changed
policy (using §12.8 and stability::implicit/dependencies closures), derived values or refusal dependencies require existing paired-world
replay or remain uncovered. Equal scalar values alone are not source provenance.
Hidden/omitted payloads and safe projections must not create a failure.
For a typed returned row, all row-local policy operands (including reference
source columns) must be present and equal to the judged committed image. A
saved readable row returned after its owner changes is uncovered, not refuted
against the new owner. External rule/read-authority dependencies must remain
unchanged through the transitive closure. The captured server clock is injected
into instrumented judgment images as in existing fixture_policy replay.
Declared read intent constrains actual typed output even for insertion echoes;
a creator's supplied or copied values are not an implicit privacy exemption.
Three prior query-coupling controls returned their protected field from a writer;
retain their query intent but project writer ids, and separately assert the old
full-row returns now refute. No weakening of the independent invariant. The reviewed direct runtime reductions are WriteTarget's final ordinary get
followed by columns::write_result, and insertion's ProjectFields builtin using
project_row_if_readable with column rules. Explicit return expressions consume
already projected authored values. Refusal and derived paths are not claimed to
be universally reduced by those two cases: paired observers and explicit blockers
remain. Regress both server insertion and server update result redaction. This
work never treats a query projection as an authorization rule.

The insert-intent and result-exposure review notes remain visible in short `check` output outside the ordinary note cap, following the existing escalation precedent. Nullable creation arguments retain null-first population coverage; direct optional column-write challenges interleave non-null alternatives within the existing finite cap. Missing commit-end images from expiry report coverage only and never stand in for an executed delete.

### 15.3 Rule-changing deployment review

Both deploy and deploy --check lead with outstanding security review items when
bound row/column/owner rules change against the selected live release (including
added/deleted tables). Print every refuted/unproved declaration and each security
note without an ordinary note cap, with source, reason, repair/next command.
Display the last recorded test time, age, completion, reachable violations and
coverage gaps; identify stale source/fixture/CLI evidence explicitly. No record
means never tested, not passed. Matching tests remain finite evidence, not proof.
Timestamp metadata is outside the input fingerprint. Record latest-run time and
outcome separately from last-completed-campaign time; an incomplete run may update
the former but never refresh the latter. Persist optional timestamps with
existing input-bound records;
legacy records say age unknown rather than inventing a date. A later incomplete
run cannot erase earlier established refutations on the same inputs.

Ordinary deploy remains advisory. Explicit --strict refuses rule-changing
previews and activation on failure-eligible reachable refutations (fresh bounded
analysis or matching persisted evidence), before any activation. Analysis, persisted evidence and narration resolve the
same saved-source companion fixtures once (an explicit --fixture overrides). Unproved items,
notes, stale evidence and synthetic-only refutations are not strict refusals.
Matching persisted eligible failures suffice even when bounded resampling is
skipped; skipped work is never described as fresh exploration. Refutations lead
the text; unproved declarations are one line per target and notes follow, avoiding
large per-obligation expansions. Keep all items visible as the author requested.
No stricter meaning is attached to passing finite tests. JSON retains the review
text and stderr prominently prints it; no preview/private snapshot mutates live
release data. Review follows the selected saved source and fixtures.

### 15.4 Supplied client sessions

Online and local constructors validate a supplied session before any transport,
device/storage work or timer. Require a non-array object with nonempty principal,
kind and token strings, a positive finite safe-integer expiresAt in milliseconds,
and valid optional scope/capExpiresAt fields. Legacy scope-less sessions remain
accepted. Expiry relative to a possibly skewed local clock is server authority;
shape validation must not reject a structurally valid past timestamp. Malformed
input throws E_SESSION with the offending field and a repair: pass the complete
Session returned by guest/login/signup or service minting, or use the existing
online token option for a bare bearer. Never include credentials in the error.
capExpiresAt without scope remains accepted legacy metadata; neither grants read authority.
Stored-session validation retains its existing auth restore path. Snapshot validated primitive fields so caller mutation cannot manufacture a NaN
timer later. Existing intentional full-session refresh and read/action expiry
semantics remain unchanged; shape validation grants no authority.

### 15.5 Qualification

Regress insert/upsert/if-absent and multi-write/rollback/role-change boundaries,
optional non-null samples, mutation payload/privacy safe controls, strict deploy
and evidence-age/staleness, malformed sessions before side effects and valid
legacy/read/action sessions. Re-measure copies of seven wave-21 lanes, both
without invariants (advice) and with spec intent plus test (replayed catches).
The seven lanes are 001,006,023,033,036,040,048, plus 048's carried-over
riders.read finding. Freeze copied .q bytes and their SHA256 manifest under
reports/20261021-sec6 before experiments; raw apps remain read-only. Report a
per-finding flagged/caught/partial/not-caught table, never conflating advice and
replay. Optional arguments must include null and a non-null typed value, with a
non-null riderGoals insert witness. The false-refusal corpus is the user-requested
waves 15,16,18–21; wave17 can be supplementary but is not silently part of that bar.
Record new advice counts and specification-safe controls separately from refusals.
Measure false refusals on waves 15,16,18–21; do not label conditional advice as
a caught exploit. Packed guide qualification executes changed examples as written.

## 16. SEC7: advice that reaches the builder

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22. Wave-22 commission;
DX6 owns session/upgrade/clock and frontend/proof-kit fixes. No version bump.
This section supersedes §15.2's special display cap for insert/result notes and
§12.3's advisory-only boundary only for the two explicit gates below.
It also revises §§13.5/14.2/14.5/14.6: disposition-clearable review gates are
a new exit-1 category alongside replayed intent violations, including without
invariants. Their zero-new-refusal bars continue to bind failures no disposition
can clear (compilation failures and reachable refutations). Gate counts and
legitimate review costs are reported separately, never called replayed exploits. Runtime
authority and the proved/refuted/unproved contract remain unchanged.

### 16.1 Presentation and conditional questions

A shared severity ordering puts gated notes and likely-hole questions before
coverage, restates and result inventories. Default check selects the first max(5, number of priority 0–2 notes)
items in severity order, including every likely-hole question; SEC6 insert/result notes never increase
that cap. Result exposure notes group by source table, retaining every operation,
column, observer kind and source site in JSON/full text. Audit uses the same
severity and numeric file/line/column ordering, never lexicographic site strings.
Insert-write advice requires an explicitly supplied nonliteral expression whose
flow uses a mutation argument; omitted defaults and literal-only assignments are
quiet. These are precision changes to advice, never creation-policy inference.

New advisory classes ask about: reason text without a positive non-whitespace
minimum; optional money supplied to creation; constant true payment/approval
flags (a naming heuristic, explicitly a question); competing generic enum setters
versus stricter literal transitions; mutable creation checks omitted by a later
transition or derived creation; inconsistent server-now duration bounds on the
same bound column across operations; and global-role operations recording viewer
on an unrelated row. Restating questions and the draft ask whether each role may
write any row, independently of the read questionnaire. The relationship recipe
models the owner of this wall explicitly. These shapes cannot infer the spec.

### 16.2 Narrow gates and individual dispositions

N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE: an optional image/file mutation argument flows to an
insert/upsert of a media column, including a conditional child insertion. A
non-null guard around just the child does not establish required parent evidence.
An unconditional dominating require of non-null before any creating write can
exclude the note; aliases/helpers are resolved by the existing authority walker.
Optionality may be intentional: the gate requires review, never asserts an exploit.

N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT: a read branch trusts a retained grant; an authored grant
writer requires a literal role on the principal argument stored as that grant's
reader principal; that role is mutable through authored operations; and the read
branch does not imply the same current-reader role. Require a positive recipient
role atom in every admitting writer branch; do not mistake the assigner's role
for the recipient's. The gate excludes ordinary owner branches, literal service
seats and mere caller-role requirements. Revoking derived grants is a repair;
static inability to prove full revocation remains reviewable, not an automatic
safety theorem. General N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE stays advisory.

Unreviewed gates make check exit 1 with structured note IDs, without marking an
invariant refuted. The existing bulk --ack-notes cannot clear a security gate.
Use check --ack-security <id> --reason '<spec rationale>' for one current note.
Store versioned dispositions in snapback/security-reviews.json with ID, reason,
source digest and analyzer revision. IDs hash the complete diagnostic; the source
digest hashes all compiled source filenames and bytes in sorted order. Every
source edit, changed diagnostic or analyzer revision invalidates the disposition.
Blank reasons, unknown IDs, malformed files and incompatible flag combinations
refuse. Writes use temp+rename and verify compiled sources still match disk;
failed compilation/refuted intent must never record acknowledgments. Dispositions
are review records, never authority or proof, remain visible in JSON/audit and
can be checked into source control. Broad advice may also be individually reviewed.

### 16.3 Directed finite histories

Keep the 512 breadth/pair budget. Add a separate bounded directed frontier before
breadth exploration for schema enum transitions and mutable dependencies of
independent intent/creator checks. Generate successors from the current world,
so references returned/created at one step are available later. Track semantic
state signatures over status/role/boolean/relationship fields, rather than minted
IDs alone; prioritize unseen lifecycle signatures and schema-relevant mutations.
Bound depth to eight authored mutations, attempts to 2048 and retained frontier
to 16 worlds; share check's existing work/deadline cap and report every cut.
Actual mutation admission, executed-write judgments and ordinary observers remain
the only failure oracle. Synthetic witnesses remain REVIEW and never failures.
Validated generated initial fixtures retain their explicit provenance. No seed
state is relabeled application reachable, and finite exhaustion remains unproved.

### 16.4 Qualification

Regression tests cover positive and negative shapes, helper/alias guards,
per-note/source/analyzer invalidation, failed-check non-writing, severity and
numeric ordering, and lifecycle/suspension histories beyond pairs. Measure every
wave-22 schema's default visibility and waves 15,16,18–22's gate counts separately
from false refusals. Optional evidence can legitimately fail pending a disposition;
report that cost explicitly rather than claim every gate is a confirmed hole.
Re-run copies of the eight high lanes without intent, with authored intent, and
with one independent spec-worded invariant. Advice is flagged; only a replayed
violation is caught. Packed qualification executes every changed guide snippet.

### 16.5 Review clarification: exact detector and command boundaries

These details resolve design review M1–M6. The implementation is the bounded
shape detector, not an inference that a schema satisfies its product specification.

Priority codes are fixed by security::note_priority: gates at 0; privilege and
self-claimed relationship at 1; grant-outlives, optional evidence, role-wide read,
broad read, caller time, sweep deadline and the seven new question codes at 2;
ordinary notes at 4; insert/result inventory at 5; restates at 6; intent coverage
at 7. The short cap is max(5, count at 0–2), or fewer if fewer exist. Concrete
reachable refutations still print all failing sites, origins and repair commands
outside this list. A remainder count points to verbose/JSON. JSON retains every
note. Restates and coverage can appear only after the action classes fit.

Creation reason: an argument flows into an inserted/upserted column with `reason`
in its case-insensitive name and its text argument minimum is absent or zero.
Optional money: the optional argument flows into an optional Money column, or an
Int/IntRange/Decimal column whose lowercased name contains price, charge, fee or
amount. Constant approval: true literal assigned to a creating column containing
paid, approv or payment. All are name-based specification questions; no claim
covers other spellings. Required positive-minimum reasons and required money
arguments suppress their respective advice. One note per operation/column.

Transition bypass: same-table enum update from an enum argument can choose a
literal also written by another mutation, and its necessary effective admission
does not imply that other writer's bound requirements plus table gate. Resolve
row-local read operands to the target table's focal row for this advisory query.
A dominating contradictory literal argument guard excludes that target. Generic
setter/table/target/stricter-op duplicates collapse by exact diagnostic message.
A stricter-gate failure alone is advisory, never an inferred approval violation.
Stale creation: a resolved creating-write context reads a non-role dependency
that another mutation updates/upserts/deletes, and a different later mutation
updates the created table or writes a referencing child without reading that
dependency in its dominating context. Group by operation, created table and
dependency. Mere dependency reads do not prove eligibility; this heuristic's
false negatives and speculative questions are not gated.
Duration: compare the same resolved table/column against now +/- literal duration
with the same inequality direction in two operation contexts. Different offsets
produce one question listing all windows/operations. Other affine expressions,
custom wrappers and complex derived windows remain uncovered by this advice.
Role-wide write: a mutation updates a non-immutable Principal to viewer, has a
bound global role requirement, and lacks a dominating viewer-to-row-member
relationship comparison. It asks about this resource rather than declaring an
unauthorized write. The draft is bounded by discovered schema roles × tables;
it asks every pair about write authority, including pairs with authored intent.

Optional upload: the existing lexical authority walker resolves aliases and
helper arguments to the original mutation argument. Non-null suppression accepts
only a direct positive `argument != null` conjunct in an unconditional Require
before the first creating-write context. `$advice` branch guards cannot suppress;
late child guards, OR, unknown and unsupported expressions cannot suppress.
Known flows into asset fields are detected; arbitrary opaque/derived flows are
uncovered, never assumed safe. Both upsert arms retain the creation review question.
Retained role: use existing lints::mutable_role and privilege role-column/probe
resolution. Only direct positive Exists conjuncts with a literal recipient role
qualify, never an assigner/viewer-only test or negation. A branch suppresses only
when kernel::implies(branch, recipient-role-with-viewer) is Ok(true); false,
unknown and cap exhaustion retain the question. Dynamic role requirements stay
in general grant advice and are outside the narrow gate. Every noted branch
contains a positive grant probe binding that grant's principal column to viewer.
Owner equalities without such probes are excluded structurally. Role mutation
plus transactional grant revocation still needs review; it is not statically
proved safe or automatically refused as an exploit.

Disposition format is version 1: entries maps full diagnostic SHA256 IDs to
{sources, analyzer, reason}. JSON uses the existing Diagnostic serialization and
the sorted Vec<(filename, UTF-8 source bytes)> serialization used by Project.sources;
source names are schema-directory-relative (`name.q`). A line shift changes the
ID; any source edit changes all dispositions. This intentionally conservative
invalidation avoids unmodeled cross-table influence; optimization is deferred.
Analyzer is the explicit security_reviews::ANALYZER revision, bumped whenever
security detector meaning or disposition identity changes. A release alone does
not invalidate unchanged analysis. A reason must contain non-whitespace text.
No unrelated refuted invariant can be bypassed: any reachable refutation prevents
all acknowledgement writes and check still exits 1. Compilation errors likewise
write no security review. Read-only inspection remains available. Bulk --ack-notes
never addresses these records. --ack-security requires --reason and refuses
combination with --ack-notes, --fix, --store, --release, --emit-lean or --audit;
--json, --verbose, --cost and current-source fixture/residency inspection may
combine. Broad-advice dispositions change display, never exit status. Audit exits
on refuted intent as before and includes all notes/dispositions; new gates fail
check only. Deploy/preview/--strict keep §15.3; we recommend running check in acceptance before
deploy. Saved-source portability is not claimed for dispositions.

Directed exploration is primarily a test campaign: check retains the common
512-work/1500ms cap and may cut before it. No promise grants both directed and
breadth paths their full caps under check. Test retains its 512 singles/pairs
in addition to the directed bound. Successors fork the current complete fixture
through Engine::fork_fixture with its asset ledger; they use ordinary mutate,
NOW and existing executed/observer judgments. The signature keeps enum/bool/
principal/reference fields (with multiplicity), discarding other payload only as
a pruning heuristic. Such mergers and every frontier/argument/successor/depth cut
are explicitly uncovered. Retain up to four priority worlds (below the 16 maximum),
prefer changed enum states and true booleans, at most two new worlds per op/world,
four argument tuples, eight mutations and 2048 actual attempts. These finite
heuristics neither prove reachability nor clear an unproved verdict. Synthetic
matrix probes stay REVIEW; ordinary admitted histories from validated generated
initial fixtures retain GeneratedFixtureTrace and failure eligibility (§14).

Wave-22 high lanes are 004,011,021,026,030,037,040,044. Frozen source copies and
independent spec variants are in tests/security/wave22; the measurement manifest
records SHA256s. 037 remains the specification judgment case. With no invariants,
expect corresponding new/advisory notes on the seven nonjudgment lanes. Spec tests
must catch 026's terminal pickup and 030's suspend-before-approval history, alongside
existing 004/021/037 catches; 040/044 always/absent-money limits stay explicit.
Gate calibration targets: 021 for retained role; 003,018,027,040 for optional
uploads on wave22. 027 may be legitimate optionality: gate cost is review pending,
not a confirmed hole. Across waves15,16,18–22 report every project that fails a gate,
all compilation/refutation refusals separately, and every legitimate gate needing
a disposition. Zero false *unacknowledgeable* refusals is the acceptance criterion;
never relabel legitimate review gates zero false positives. Changed executable
guide material: wall-ownership Quarry in rule-recipes; existing cheatsheet,
security and upload-placement snippets are rerun from the fresh packed client.

### 16.6 Second-review resolutions

The two new gates are check-CI-only review obligations. Deploy still lists all
security notes in §15.3 review; its --strict boundary remains replayed refutations.
Running check in deployment acceptance is recommended, not an enforced fresh-check
receipt protocol. No saved-source deployment claim is made for these dispositions.
Acknowledged gates plus no other errors/refutations give exit 0. Default output
replaces each acknowledged note with its ID/code/reason review record; verbose,
JSON and audit retain the full note. Broad dispositions have this same display
effect with no exit-code effect. Stale entries remain in the file for review
history but do not acknowledge any current note. `check --json`, `check --verbose`
and audit inspect current notes without writing a disposition; malformed files
refuse with repair text. There is no wildcard or bulk security acknowledgment.

IDs use deterministic recursively key-sorted JSON diagnostic values; source digest
uses sorted source pairs. Tests require same-input/same-ID stability on repeated
checks in addition to invalidation controls. The analyzer revision is a reviewed
constant; changes to detector semantics or fingerprint serialization must bump it
and their tests must reject a previous revision. It is not a runtime security
boundary or promise that arbitrary modified binaries honor review semantics.

Transition advice emits only on definite Ok(false) implication, suppressing unknown
or capped comparisons as uncovered by that heuristic. Stale-creation, reason,
money, constant-approval and duration advice use finite structural matches only:
unknown/unsupported syntax does not match and is not inferred safe. Their documented
naming/type/binding fragment is their entire coverage, without a proof claim.
General retained-grant advice and the narrow gate may co-fire intentionally:
count gate projects by gated code and project, not total note count. Neither
advisory repeat creates another required disposition. This preserves existing
conditional-lifetime explanations while identifying the precise required review.

Successor generation, schema/program/actor ordering, argument tuples and stable
priority ties are deterministic for fixed inputs. Time/work cuts remain explicit.
Root generated populations are visited before derived creators mutate their
status/eligibility. This is a validated generated initial fixture, with the same
explicit GeneratedFixtureTrace origin; no root rows are invented terminal states.
The additional pre-transition pass is full-test-only, shares the campaign's
observer ceilings, and never runs recursively or increases check's budget.

SEC7 design review provenance: [verbatim reviews](./reviews/3000.036.004-sec7-review-record.md).

Implementation-review clarifications: upload suppression intersects the positive
non-null requirements of every creating-write context, rather than borrowing one
branch's guards. Recipient probes distinguish the original viewer from the stored
recipient, verify the roster principal column and retain only its literal role
requirement for current-role comparison. Result identity adds to existing source
lineage; it never replaces copied protected-column provenance. Role-wide-write
suppression requires a structural positive viewer-to-target relationship equality.
Directed search includes mutable role/boolean/relationship dependencies, uses two
prioritized actors per tuple (others remain in breadth), and immediately drops
displaced beam worlds. The retained-world high-water mark and shared directed
attempt counter are reported. Disposition writers serialize read-modify-write
through a kernel file lock in ignored generated output, with automatic release

//! Creation and transition risk questions, independent of authored invariants.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#162-narrow-gates-and-individual-dispositions — these gates demand review, never infer a specification.
use super::*;
use ast::{Expr, Stmt, WriteOp, ProgramKind, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
fn note(out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>,site:&Site,code:&str,message:String,repair:&str){out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,code,message,repair).guide("security"));}
fn walk(e:&Expr,mut f:impl FnMut(&Expr)){let mut e=e.clone();super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut e,&mut |e|f(e));}
fn uses(e:&Expr,arg:&str)->bool{let mut found=false;walk(e,|e|if matches!(e,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==arg){found=true;});found}
fn conjunction(e:&Expr)->Vec<&Expr>{match e{Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="and"=>conjunction(left).into_iter().chain(conjunction(right)).collect(),_=>vec![e]}}
fn conditions(p:&ast::ProgramDecl)->Vec<&Expr>{p.body.iter().filter_map(|s|if let Stmt::Require{condition,..}=s{Some(condition)}else{None}).collect()}
fn reads(e:&Expr)->BTreeSet<String>{let mut out=BTreeSet::new();walk(e,|e|match e{Expr::Read(r)|Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=>{out.insert(r.table.clone());},_=>{}});out}
fn nonnull(p:&ast::ProgramDecl,arg:&str)->bool{p.body.iter().filter_map(|s|if let Stmt::Require{condition,code,..}=s&&code!="$advice"{Some(condition)}else{None}).flat_map(conjunction).any(|e|match e{
    Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="!="=>[(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref())].iter().any(|(a,b)|matches!(a,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==arg)&&matches!(b,Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Null,..})),_=>false,
})}
fn field_value<'a>(f:&'a ast::ObjectField)->Option<&'a Expr>{f.value.as_ref()}
struct Write<'a>{p:&'a ast::ProgramDecl,op:WriteOp,table:String,fields:Vec<ast::ObjectField>,site:Site,context:ast::ProgramDecl,gate:RuleExpr}
fn bound_role(bound:&Bound,catalog:&Catalog,info:&super::super::catalog::TableInfo,expr:&Expr,recipient:&str)->Option<RuleExpr>{
    let Expr::Exists{read,..}=expr else{return None;};
    // Bind a positive recipient probe after substituting only its principal.
    if !read.keys.iter().any(|k|matches!(&k.value,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==recipient)){return None;}
    let mut value=expr.clone();super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value,&mut |e|if let Expr::Name{name,site}=e{
        if name=="viewer"{*e=Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str("service:security-assigner-marker".into()),site:site.clone()};}
        else if name==recipient{*name="viewer".into();}
    });
    let rule=super::super::schema::bind_rule(catalog,info,&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),site:expr.site().clone(),value}).ok()?;
    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=&rule else{return None;};
    let principal=super::privilege::global_principal(bound,table)?;
    let pairs=super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    if !pairs.iter().any(|(c,v)|c==&principal&&*v==RuleTerm::Viewer){return None;}
    let roles=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table);
    let equalities=pairs.into_iter().filter(|(c,v)|roles.contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_)))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if equalities.is_empty(){return None;}
    let index=bound.schema.tables[table].indexes.iter().find(|(_,i)|i.unique&&i.columns()==vec![principal.clone()])?.0.clone();
    Some(RuleExpr::Exists{table:table.clone(),index,key:vec![RuleTerm::Viewer],equalities,provenance:snapback4_core::ir::KeyProvenance::Computed})

}
pub(super) fn notes(sources:&[(String,String)],bound:&Bound)->Vec<Diagnostic>{
    let docs=sources.iter().map(|(file,text)|crate::parse_document(file,text).0).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let catalog=Catalog::build(docs.clone(),&mut Vec::new());let mut writes=Vec::new();
    for p in docs.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation){
        for (write,context) in super::authority_walk::writes(&catalog,p){
            let Expr::Write{op,table,fields,site,..}=write else{continue;};
            let Some(info)=catalog.tables.get(&table)else{continue;};
            let mut requirements=super::operation::requirements(&catalog,info,&context);
            for condition in conditions(&context){let mut value=condition.clone();
                super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value,&mut |e|if let Expr::Member{base,..}=e&&reads(base).contains(&table){*base=Box::new(Expr::Focal{site:site.clone()});});
                if let Ok(rule)=super::super::schema::bind_rule(&catalog,info,&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value,site:site.clone()}){requirements.push(rule);}
            }
            let t=&bound.schema.tables[&table];let rule=if op==WriteOp::Update{t.update_rule_judged().into_owned()}else{t.rules.insert.clone()};
            writes.push(Write{p,op,table,fields,site,context,gate:RuleExpr::And(requirements.into_iter().chain([rule]).collect())});
        }
    }
    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
    for w in &writes{
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&w.table];let creating=matches!(w.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert);
        if creating{
            // Only unconditional requirements before the FIRST creating write
            // establish mandatory parent evidence; a conditional child's guard cannot.
            let creating_contexts=writes.iter().filter(|a|std::ptr::eq(a.p,w.p)&&matches!(a.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            for f in &w.fields{let Some(value)=field_value(f)else{continue;};let Some(kind)=t.columns.get(&f.name)else{continue;};
                for arg in &w.p.args{if !uses(value,&arg.name){continue;}
                    if arg.type_.optional&&matches!(arg.type_.kind,TypeKind::Image{..}|TypeKind::File{..})&&kind.contains_asset()&&!creating_contexts.iter().all(|a|nonnull(&a.context,&arg.name))&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("upload:{}",arg.name))){
                        note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE",format!("{}: optional {} can omit media while creating {}; including conditional child evidence, does the spec require this upload?",w.p.name,arg.name,w.table),"make the creating mutation's upload argument required, or require it before creating the parent; intentional optionality needs an individual security disposition");
                    }
                    if f.name.to_lowercase().contains("reason")&&matches!(arg.type_.kind,TypeKind::Text{min:None|Some(0),..})&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("reason:{}",f.name))){
                        note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CREATION_REASON",format!("{}: {}.{} accepts an empty/whitespace reason at creation",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"if the workflow requires justification, use text 1..N (non-whitespace minimum), including on optional reasons when supplied");
                    }
                    let money=matches!(kind.required(),ColumnType::Money{..})||["price","charge","fee","amount"].iter().any(|word|f.name.to_lowercase().contains(word))&&matches!(kind.required(),ColumnType::Int|ColumnType::IntRange{..}|ColumnType::Decimal{..});
                    if money&&arg.type_.optional&&kind.is_optional()&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("money:{}",f.name))){note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CREATION_MONEY",format!("{}: {}.{} permits omitted money at creation; is the required amount recorded?",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"model the amount and require it at the workflow boundary when the spec requires payment or a price");}
                }
                if matches!(value,Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Bool(true),..})&&["paid","approv","payment"].iter().any(|s|f.name.to_lowercase().contains(s)){
                    note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CONSTANT_APPROVAL",format!("{}: {}.{} is stored as constant true; what verifies the payment/approval and its amount? (name-based question)",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"record the required money/evidence and independently authorized decision; confirm constant flags against the spec");
                }
            }
        }
        // A global role plus viewer attribution does not model ownership of this resource.
        if w.op==WriteOp::Update&&w.fields.iter().any(|f|matches!(field_value(f),Some(Expr::Name{name,..}) if name=="viewer")&&matches!(t.columns.get(&f.name).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Principal))){
            let safe=RuleExpr::And(conditions(&w.context).into_iter().filter_map(|e|super::super::schema::bind_rule(&catalog,&catalog.tables[&w.table],&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value:e.clone(),site:e.site().clone()}).ok()).filter(super::operation::safe).collect());
            let has_role=kernel::dependencies(&safe).iter().any(|dep|!super::privilege::role_columns(bound,dep).is_empty());
            let relationship=conditions(&w.context).iter().any(|e|conjunction(e).iter().any(|e|if let Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..}=e&&op=="="{
                [(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref())].iter().any(|(a,b)|matches!(a,Expr::Name{name,..} if name=="viewer")&&matches!(b,Expr::Member{base,..} if reads(base).contains(&w.table)))
            }else{false}));
            if has_role&&!relationship&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),"wide-write".into())){note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE",format!("{}: may any admitted role write any {} row and record themselves? Does the spec mean owner of THIS resource?",w.p.name,w.table),"model the resource-to-owner principal/reference relationship and require it for this row; a global owner role is not ownership of every resource");}
        }
        // A grant's stored recipient, not its assigner, must have the role.
        if creating||w.op==WriteOp::Update{
            for f in &w.fields{if !matches!(t.columns.get(&f.name).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Principal)){continue;}
                let Some(Expr::Name{name:recipient,..})=field_value(f)else{continue;};
                if recipient=="viewer"||!w.p.args.iter().any(|a|a.name==*recipient&&matches!(a.type_.kind,TypeKind::Principal)){continue;}
                for condition in conditions(&w.context).into_iter().flat_map(conjunction){
                    let Some(role)=bound_role(bound,&catalog,&catalog.tables[&w.table],condition,recipient)else{continue;};
                    let RuleExpr::Exists{table:roster,index,key,equalities,..}=&role else{continue;};
                    let role_columns=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,roster);
                    if !super::lints::mutable_role(bound,roster)||!super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,roster,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns.contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_)))){continue;}
                    for (name,target) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for branch in target.rules.read.disjuncts(){
                        let trusts=positive_probes(branch).iter().any(|probe|if let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=probe{table==&w.table&&super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|c==&f.name&&*v==RuleTerm::Viewer)}else{false});
                        if trusts&&kernel::implies(branch,&role)!=Ok(true)&&seen.insert((name.clone(),format!("grant:{}:{}",w.table,role.quarry()))){note(&mut out,&Site::default(),"N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT",format!("{name}.read: retained {} grants are written by {} requiring recipient `{}`, but read branch `{}` does not recheck that mutable current role",w.table,w.p.name,role.quarry(),branch.quarry()),"conjoin the current recipient role at read time, or revoke derived grants on every role change; individually acknowledge intentional retained authority");}
                    }}
                }
            }
        }
    }
    transition_notes(bound,&catalog,&writes,&mut out);
    duration_notes(&writes,&mut out);
    let mut dedup=BTreeSet::new();out.retain(|n|dedup.insert((n.code.clone(),n.message.clone())));out
}
fn positive_probes(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<&RuleExpr>{match rule{RuleExpr::And(xs)=>xs.iter().flat_map(positive_probes).collect(),RuleExpr::Exists{..}=>vec![rule],_=>vec![]}}
fn transition_notes(bound:&Bound,_catalog:&Catalog,writes:&[Write<'_>],out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>){
    for w in writes{let t=&bound.schema.tables[&w.table];
        if w.op==WriteOp::Update{for f in &w.fields{
            let Some(ColumnType::Enum{..})=t.columns.get(&f.name).map(ColumnType::required)else{continue;};
            let Some(Expr::Name{name,..})=field_value(f)else{continue;};
            let Some(arg)=w.p.args.iter().find(|a|a.name==*name)else{continue;};let TypeKind::Enum{variants}=&arg.type_.kind else{continue;};
            for other in writes.iter().filter(|o|o.op==WriteOp::Update&&o.table==w.table&&o.p.name!=w.p.name){
                let mut targets=BTreeSet::new();for g in other.fields.iter().filter(|g|g.name==f.name){if let Some(value)=field_value(g){walk(value,|e|if let Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str(v),..}=e{targets.insert(v.clone());});}}
                for target in &targets{
                    if variants.contains(target)&&!conditions(&w.context).iter().any(|e|super::lints::argument_guard(e,name,target)==Some(false))&&kernel::implies(&w.gate,&other.gate)==Ok(false){note(out,&w.site,"N_TRANSITION_BYPASS",format!("{}: generic {}.{} setter includes '{target}', also set by stricter {} ({})",w.p.name,w.table,f.name,other.p.name,other.gate.quarry()),"enforce the decision's authority on every path reaching this status; declare independent transition/column update intent and run test");}
                }
            }
        }}
        if !matches!(w.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert){continue;}
        let deps=conditions(&w.context).iter().flat_map(|e|reads(e)).filter(|dep|dep!=&w.table&&super::privilege::role_columns(bound,dep).is_empty()&&writes.iter().any(|o|&o.table==dep&&o.op!=WriteOp::Insert)).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
        for dep in deps{for later in writes.iter().filter(|o|o.p.name!=w.p.name&&(o.table==w.table&&o.op==WriteOp::Update||bound.schema.tables[&o.table].columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),ColumnType::Ref{table} if table==&w.table)))){
            if conditions(&later.context).iter().any(|e|reads(e).contains(&dep)){continue;}
            note(out,&later.site,"N_STALE_CREATION_CHECK",format!("{}: {} creation by {} checks mutable {dep}, but this later {} write does not recheck it",later.p.name,w.table,w.p.name,later.table),"if eligibility must remain current at approval/transition, recheck it there; declare independent intent and test a create -> eligibility change -> approval history");
        }}
    }
}

fn duration_number(e:&Expr)->Option<i64>{
    let Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Number(s),..}=e else{return None;};
    for (suffix,scale) in [("ms",1),("s",1000),("m",60_000),("h",3_600_000),("d",86_400_000)]{
        if let Some(n)=s.strip_suffix(suffix){return n.parse::<i64>().ok()?.checked_mul(scale);}
    }s.parse().ok()
}
fn duration_notes(writes:&[Write<'_>],out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>){
    let mut bounds=BTreeMap::<(String,String,String),BTreeMap<i64,BTreeSet<String>>>::new();
    let mut sites=BTreeMap::new();
    for w in writes{for condition in conditions(&w.context){walk(condition,|e|{
        let Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..}=e else{return;};if !matches!(op.as_str(),">"|">="|"<"|"<="){return;}
        for (field,clock,cmp) in [(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref(),op.as_str()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref(),match op.as_str(){">"=>"<",">="=>"<=","<"=>">",_=>">="})]{
            let Expr::Binary{op:offset,left:now,right:amount,..}=clock else{continue;};
            if !matches!(now.as_ref(),Expr::Name{name,..} if name=="now")||!matches!(offset.as_str(),"-"|"+"){continue;}
            let Some(mut duration)=duration_number(amount)else{continue;};if offset=="-"{duration=-duration;}
            let target=match field{
                Expr::Member{base,name,..}=>reads(base).into_iter().next().map(|table|(table,name.clone())),
                Expr::Name{name,..}=>writes.iter().filter(|a|a.p.name==w.p.name).find_map(|a|a.fields.iter().find(|f|field_value(f).is_some_and(|e|matches!(e,Expr::Name{name:n,..} if n==name))).map(|f|(a.table.clone(),f.name.clone()))),_=>None,
            };
            if let Some((table,column))=target{let key=(table,column,cmp.to_string());sites.entry(key.clone()).or_insert_with(||w.site.clone());bounds.entry(key).or_default().entry(duration).or_default().insert(w.p.name.clone());}
        }
    });}}
    for ((table,column,cmp),values) in bounds{if values.len()<2{continue;}
        let entries=values.iter().map(|(ms,ops)|format!("now {ms:+}ms in {}",ops.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ");
        note(out,&sites[&(table.clone(),column.clone(),cmp.clone())],"N_DURATION_BOUNDS",format!("{table}.{column}: inconsistent `{cmp}` duration bounds across operations: {entries}; are these different windows intentional?"),"use the specification's duration consistently at creation, renewal and approval, or document the deliberate workflow difference");
    }
}
//! Individually justified source-bound dispositions; never proof or authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#162-narrow-gates-and-individual-dispositions — bulk compiler-note review cannot clear security gates.
use super::*;
use serde::{Serialize,Deserialize};
use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};
const ANALYZER:&str="sec7-2";
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct File{version:u32,entries:BTreeMap<String,Entry>}
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Entry{sources:String,analyzer:String,reason:String}
#[derive(Serialize)]
pub(super) struct Disposition{pub id:String,pub code:String,pub site:String,pub gated:bool,pub acknowledged:bool,pub reason:Option<String>}
pub(super) fn gated(code:&str)->bool{matches!(code,"N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT"|"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE")}
fn hash(bytes:&[u8])->String{format!("{:x}",Sha256::digest(bytes))}
fn digest(s:&str)->bool{s.len()==64&&s.bytes().all(|b|b.is_ascii_hexdigit())}
fn id(n:&snapback4_lang::Diagnostic)->String{hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&serde_json::to_value(n).unwrap()).unwrap())}
pub(super) fn review(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>)->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    if project.sources().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=sources{return Err("Quarry sources changed during security review; rerun check".into());}
    let mut sorted=sources.to_vec();sorted.sort_by(|a,b|a.0.cmp(&b.0));
    let fingerprint=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&sorted).unwrap());
    let path=project.schema_dir().join("security-reviews.json");
    // Keep the lock inode in ignored generated output. Do not unlink a lock
    // while another process can be waiting on that inode. Kernel ownership
    // releases it on exit, including an interrupted writer.
    let _writer_lock=if ack.is_some(){
        let directory=project.schema_dir().join("generated");
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&directory).map_err(|e|format!("cannot create security review lock directory: {e}"))?;
        let lock=std::fs::OpenOptions::new().create(true).truncate(false).read(true).write(true).open(directory.join("security-reviews.lock")).map_err(|e|format!("cannot open security review lock: {e}"))?;
        lock.lock().map_err(|e|format!("cannot acquire security review lock: {e}"))?;Some(lock)
    }else{None};
    let mut file:File=match std::fs::read(&path){
        Ok(bytes)=>serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|_|"invalid snapback/security-reviews.json; expected version 1 with per-note source/analyzer/reason records".to_string())?,
        Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>File{version:1,entries:BTreeMap::new()},
        Err(e)=>return Err(format!("cannot read security dispositions: {e}")),
    };
    if file.version!=1||file.entries.iter().any(|(key,e)|!digest(key)||!digest(&e.sources)||e.reason.trim().is_empty()||e.analyzer.is_empty()){return Err("invalid security disposition version/digest/reason".into());}
    if let Some((key,reason))=ack{
        if reason.trim().is_empty(){return Err("--reason needs the specification rationale for this individual note".into());}
        if !notes.iter().any(|n|id(n)==key){return Err("--ack-security ID is not a current security note; run check --json".into());}
        file.entries.insert(key.into(),Entry{sources:fingerprint.clone(),analyzer:ANALYZER.into(),reason:reason.trim().into()});
        let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("json.{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
        let result=std::fs::write(&temporary,serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&file).unwrap()).and_then(|_|std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path));
        if let Err(e)=result{let _=std::fs::remove_file(&temporary);return Err(format!("cannot record security disposition: {e}"));}
    }
    Ok(notes.iter().map(|n|{let key=id(n);let entry=file.entries.get(&key).filter(|e|e.sources==fingerprint&&e.analyzer==ANALYZER);
        Disposition{id:key,code:n.code.clone(),site:n.site.to_string(),gated:gated(&n.code),acknowledged:entry.is_some(),reason:entry.map(|e|e.reason.clone())}
    }).collect())
}
pub(super) fn pending(dispositions:&[Disposition])->bool{dispositions.iter().any(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged)}
pub(super) fn print(dispositions:&[Disposition]){for d in dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.gated||d.acknowledged){
    if d.acknowledged{println!("security reviewed {} {}: {}",d.id,d.code,d.reason.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));}
    else{println!("security review required {} {} at {}; repair or check --ack-security {} --reason '<spec rationale>'",d.id,d.code,d.site,d.id);}
}}
#[cfg(test)]mod tests{
use super::*;
#[test]fn individual_source_and_diagnostic_bound_dispositions(){
 let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-sec7-dispositions-{}",std::process::id()));std::fs::create_dir_all(root.join("snapback")).unwrap();
 let p=Project{root:root.clone()};std::fs::write(p.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),"query q():\n  return true\n").unwrap();let sources=p.sources().unwrap();

 assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&id(&a),"  "))).is_err());assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some(("unknown","intended"))).is_err());
 let ds=review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&id(&a),"the specification allows optional draft evidence"))).unwrap();assert!(ds[0].acknowledged);assert!(!ds[1].acknowledged);assert!(pending(&ds));
 let changed=[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","changed","")];assert!(!review(&p,&sources,&changed,None).unwrap()[0].acknowledged);
 let path=p.schema_dir().join("security-reviews.json");let mut f:File=serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(&path).unwrap()).unwrap();f.entries.get_mut(&id(&a)).unwrap().analyzer="old".into();std::fs::write(&path,serde_json::to_vec(&f).unwrap()).unwrap();assert!(!review(&p,&sources,&notes,None).unwrap()[0].acknowledged);
 std::fs::write(p.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),"query q():\n  return false\n").unwrap();assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&id(&a),"intended"))).is_err());assert!(!review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,None).unwrap()[0].acknowledged);
 std::fs::write(&path,"{}").unwrap();assert!(review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,None).is_err());std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
}
}

### 16.5 Review clarification: exact detector and command boundaries

These details resolve design review M1–M6. The implementation is the bounded
shape detector, not an inference that a schema satisfies its product specification.

Priority codes are fixed by security::note_priority: gates at 0; privilege and
self-claimed relationship at 1; grant-outlives, optional evidence, role-wide read,
broad read, caller time, sweep deadline and the seven new question codes at 2;
ordinary notes at 4; insert/result inventory at 5; restates at 6; intent coverage
at 7. The short cap is max(5, count at 0–2), or fewer if fewer exist. Concrete
reachable refutations still print all failing sites, origins and repair commands
outside this list. A remainder count points to verbose/JSON. JSON retains every
note. Restates and coverage can appear only after the action classes fit.

Creation reason: an argument flows into an inserted/upserted column with `reason`
in its case-insensitive name and its text argument minimum is absent or zero.
Optional money: the optional argument flows into an optional Money column, or an
Int/IntRange/Decimal column whose lowercased name contains price, charge, fee or
amount. Constant approval: true literal assigned to a creating column containing
paid, approv or payment. All are name-based specification questions; no claim
covers other spellings. Required positive-minimum reasons and required money
arguments suppress their respective advice. One note per operation/column.

Transition bypass: same-table enum update from an enum argument can choose a
literal also written by another mutation, and its necessary effective admission
does not imply that other writer's bound requirements plus table gate. Resolve
row-local read operands to the target table's focal row for this advisory query.
A dominating contradictory literal argument guard excludes that target. Generic
setter/table/target/stricter-op duplicates collapse by exact diagnostic message.
A stricter-gate failure alone is advisory, never an inferred approval violation.
Stale creation: a resolved creating-write context reads a non-role dependency
that another mutation updates/upserts/deletes, and a different later mutation
updates the created table or writes a referencing child without reading that
dependency in its dominating context. Group by operation, created table and
dependency. Mere dependency reads do not prove eligibility; this heuristic's
false negatives and speculative questions are not gated.
Duration: compare the same resolved table/column against now +/- literal duration
with the same inequality direction in two operation contexts. Different offsets
produce one question listing all windows/operations. Other affine expressions,
custom wrappers and complex derived windows remain uncovered by this advice.
Role-wide write: a mutation updates a non-immutable Principal to viewer, has a
bound global role requirement, and lacks a dominating viewer-to-row-member
relationship comparison. It asks about this resource rather than declaring an
unauthorized write. The draft is bounded by discovered schema roles × tables;
it asks every pair about write authority, including pairs with authored intent.

Optional upload: the existing lexical authority walker resolves aliases and
helper arguments to the original mutation argument. Non-null suppression accepts
only a direct positive `argument != null` conjunct in an unconditional Require
before the first creating-write context. `$advice` branch guards cannot suppress;
late child guards, OR, unknown and unsupported expressions cannot suppress.
Known flows into asset fields are detected; arbitrary opaque/derived flows are
uncovered, never assumed safe. Both upsert arms retain the creation review question.
Retained role: use existing lints::mutable_role and privilege role-column/probe
resolution. Only direct positive Exists conjuncts with a literal recipient role
qualify, never an assigner/viewer-only test or negation. A branch suppresses only
when kernel::implies(branch, recipient-role-with-viewer) is Ok(true); false,
unknown and cap exhaustion retain the question. Dynamic role requirements stay
in general grant advice and are outside the narrow gate. Every noted branch
contains a positive grant probe binding that grant's principal column to viewer.
Owner equalities without such probes are excluded structurally. Role mutation
plus transactional grant revocation still needs review; it is not statically
proved safe or automatically refused as an exploit.

Disposition format is version 1: entries maps full diagnostic SHA256 IDs to
{sources, analyzer, reason}. JSON uses the existing Diagnostic serialization and
the sorted Vec<(filename, UTF-8 source bytes)> serialization used by Project.sources;
source names are schema-directory-relative (`name.q`). A line shift changes the
ID; any source edit changes all dispositions. This intentionally conservative
invalidation avoids unmodeled cross-table influence; optimization is deferred.
Analyzer is the explicit security_reviews::ANALYZER revision, bumped whenever
security detector meaning or disposition identity changes. A release alone does
not invalidate unchanged analysis. A reason must contain non-whitespace text.
No unrelated refuted invariant can be bypassed: any reachable refutation prevents
all acknowledgement writes and check still exits 1. Compilation errors likewise
write no security review. Read-only inspection remains available. Bulk --ack-notes
never addresses these records. --ack-security requires --reason and refuses
combination with --ack-notes, --fix, --store, --release, --emit-lean or --audit;
--json, --verbose, --cost and current-source fixture/residency inspection may
combine. Broad-advice dispositions change display, never exit status. Audit exits
on refuted intent as before and includes all notes/dispositions; new gates fail
check only. Deploy/preview/--strict keep §15.3; we recommend running check in acceptance before
deploy. Saved-source portability is not claimed for dispositions.

Directed exploration is primarily a test campaign: check retains the common
512-work/1500ms cap and may cut before it. No promise grants both directed and
breadth paths their full caps under check. Test retains its 512 singles/pairs
in addition to the directed bound. Successors fork the current complete fixture
through Engine::fork_fixture with its asset ledger; they use ordinary mutate,
NOW and existing executed/observer judgments. The signature keeps enum/bool/
principal/reference fields (with multiplicity), discarding other payload only as
a pruning heuristic. Such mergers and every frontier/argument/successor/depth cut
are explicitly uncovered. Retain up to four priority worlds (below the 16 maximum),
prefer changed enum states and true booleans, at most two new worlds per op/world,
four argument tuples, eight mutations and 2048 actual attempts. These finite
heuristics neither prove reachability nor clear an unproved verdict. Synthetic
matrix probes stay REVIEW; ordinary admitted histories from validated generated
initial fixtures retain GeneratedFixtureTrace and failure eligibility (§14).

Wave-22 high lanes are 004,011,021,026,030,037,040,044. Frozen source copies and
independent spec variants are in tests/security/wave22; the measurement manifest
records SHA256s. 037 remains the specification judgment case. With no invariants,
expect corresponding new/advisory notes on the seven nonjudgment lanes. Spec tests
must catch 026's terminal pickup and 030's suspend-before-approval history, alongside
existing 004/021/037 catches; 040/044 always/absent-money limits stay explicit.
Gate calibration targets: 021 for retained role; 003,018,027,040 for optional
uploads on wave22. 027 may be legitimate optionality: gate cost is review pending,
not a confirmed hole. Across waves15,16,18–22 report every project that fails a gate,
all compilation/refutation refusals separately, and every legitimate gate needing
a disposition. Zero false *unacknowledgeable* refusals is the acceptance criterion;
never relabel legitimate review gates zero false positives. Changed executable
guide material: wall-ownership Quarry in rule-recipes; existing cheatsheet,
security and upload-placement snippets are rerun from the fresh packed client.

### 16.6 Second-review resolutions

The two new gates are check-CI-only review obligations. Deploy still lists all
security notes in §15.3 review; its --strict boundary remains replayed refutations.
Running check in deployment acceptance is recommended, not an enforced fresh-check
receipt protocol. No saved-source deployment claim is made for these dispositions.
Acknowledged gates plus no other errors/refutations give exit 0. Default output
replaces each acknowledged note with its ID/code/reason review record; verbose,
JSON and audit retain the full note. Broad dispositions have this same display
effect with no exit-code effect. Stale entries remain in the file for review
history but do not acknowledge any current note. `check --json`, `check --verbose`
and audit inspect current notes without writing a disposition; malformed files
refuse with repair text. There is no wildcard or bulk security acknowledgment.

IDs use deterministic recursively key-sorted JSON diagnostic values; source digest
uses sorted source pairs. Tests require same-input/same-ID stability on repeated
checks in addition to invalidation controls. The analyzer revision is a reviewed
constant; changes to detector semantics or fingerprint serialization must bump it
and their tests must reject a previous revision. It is not a runtime security
boundary or promise that arbitrary modified binaries honor review semantics.

Transition advice emits only on definite Ok(false) implication, suppressing unknown
or capped comparisons as uncovered by that heuristic. Stale-creation, reason,
money, constant-approval and duration advice use finite structural matches only:
unknown/unsupported syntax does not match and is not inferred safe. Their documented
naming/type/binding fragment is their entire coverage, without a proof claim.
General retained-grant advice and the narrow gate may co-fire intentionally:
count gate projects by gated code and project, not total note count. Neither
advisory repeat creates another required disposition. This preserves existing
conditional-lifetime explanations while identifying the precise required review.

Successor generation, schema/program/actor ordering, argument tuples and stable
priority ties are deterministic for fixed inputs. Time/work cuts remain explicit.
Root generated populations are visited before derived creators mutate their
status/eligibility. This is a validated generated initial fixture, with the same
explicit GeneratedFixtureTrace origin; no root rows are invented terminal states.
The additional pre-transition pass is full-test-only, shares the campaign's
observer ceilings, and never runs recursively or increases check's budget.

SEC7 design review provenance: [verbatim reviews](./reviews/3000.036.004-sec7-review-record.md).

Implementation-review clarifications: upload suppression intersects the positive
non-null requirements of every creating-write context, rather than borrowing one
